# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2013-10-04_TotalExtraction_37000419.pdf
1
RESOLUTION NO. _________
ENTITLED:
“A RESOLUTION AMENDING RESOLUTION NO. 5521 AND AUTHORIZING
SUBMISSION OF AN APPLICATION TO THE WYOMING BUSINESS
COUNCIL, BUSINESS READY COMMUNITY GRANT AND LOAN PROGRAM
FOR FUNDING THROUGH THE DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT GRANT AND
LOAN PROGRAM FOR AN ELEVATED WALKWAY.”
WHEREAS, Resolution No. 5521, adopted by the Governing Body at its August 26, 2013
meeting inadvertently omitted wording with respect to the loan component of the project which is
the subject of this Resolution; and
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne desires to participate in the
Wyoming Business Council Business Ready Community/Downtown Development Program to assist
Spirit Hospitality, LLC, in constructing an elevated pedestrian access walkway from the Jack R.
Spiker Parking Facility across Pioneer Avenue to a location within Block 392, Original City, City
of Cheyenne, 1620 Pioneer Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming (“Block 392”); and
WHEREAS, the purposes of the elevated pedestrian access walkway includes providing
pedestrian access for the general public from the Spiker Parking Facility to the streets, sidewalks,
and businesses located in and around the 1600 block of Pioneer Avenue and to provide access to and
from the Spiker Parking Facility for guests of a Hilton Gardens Hotel which Spirit Hospitality, LLC,
proposes to construct; and
WHEREAS, the eligibility requirements of the Business Ready Community/Downtown
Development Program include the provision that a portion of eligible program funds may be
distributed in the form of a loan to the City of Cheyenne as the grant applicant; and 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne will request Business Ready Community/Downtown
Development Program grant funds in the amount of Five Hundred Sixty-Two Thousand Two
Hundred Forty-Eight Dollars ($562,248.00) and loan funds in the amount of One Hundred Eighty-
Seven Thousand Five Hundred Dollars ($187,500.00), said loan funds to be re-paid by the City of
Cheyenne over a twenty (20) year period with simple interest in the amount of one half of one
percent (.5%) compounded annually; and
WHEREAS, pursuant to said terms and conditions, the annual debt service owed by the City
of Cheyenne to the Wyoming Business Council will be in the approximate amount of Nine
Thousand Eight Hundred Seventy-Five Dollars ($9,875.00) annually; and
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne will secure the loan through a non-exclusive lease of the
elevated pedestrian access walkway to Spirit Hospitality, LLC, for use by its guests with annual
lease payments for Years 1 through 4 in the amount of Ten Thousand Dollars ($10,000.00) and
annual lease payments for Years 5 through 20 in the amount of  Fourteen Thousand Seven Hundred
and Fifty Dollars ($14,750.00); and
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne will use revenue recaptured through this project,
estimated at Seventy-Eight Thousand Five Hundred Dollars ($78,500.00) over the twenty (20) year
lease term, to support the City of Cheyenne’s West Edge Project; and
WHEREAS, said grant and loan funds will be used by the City of Cheyenne to construct the
elevated walkway, which will be retained in the ownership of the City as public pedestrian access
infrastructure; and
WHEREAS, the use of grant and loan funds for the construction of an elevated pedestrian
access walkway satisfies the eligibility criteria which have been established by the Wyoming
Business Council for the Business Ready Community/Downtown Development Program; and

2
WHEREAS, the eligibility requirements of the Business Ready Community/Downtown
Development Program also include the requirement that the grant applicant provide a grant match
and the City’s match will be provided through the loan component of the grant application as
described above and through capital investments of Spirit Hospitality, LLC; and
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne will follow state procurement standards, inclusive of W.S.
§ 15-1-113 and W.S. § 16-6-101, et seq., in the construction of the elevated pedestrian access
walkway; and
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne will comply with the Wyoming Preference Act (W.S. §§
16-6-201 through 16-6-206) and all related state statutes in the construction of the elevated
pedestrian access walkway; and
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne recognizes the construction of an
elevated walkway will provide public benefit; and
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY
OF CHEYENNE, that a loan application in the amount of One Hundred Eighty-Seven Thousand
Five Hundred Dollars ($187,500.00), subject to and including the terms and conditions set forth
above, be submitted to the Wyoming Business Council for the purpose of assisting in the funding
of the construction of the elevated pedestrian access walkway as described above.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Mayor of the City of Cheyenne, or such other City
official as may be designated by the Mayor, is authorized to sign an application for this grant and
loan request and that the Mayor, or his designated representative, is authorized to execute a grant
agreement and loan documents as required by the Wyoming Business Council in the event that grant
and loan funds, as described herein, are awarded to the City of Cheyenne for this project.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that Resolution No. 5521 is amended to the extent that
Resolution No. 5521 is inconsistent with this Resolution.
ADOPTED THIS __________day of October, 2013.
__________________________________________
Richard L. Kaysen, Mayor
(SEAL)
Attest
____________________________________
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2013-10-04_TotalExtraction_25015984.pdf
 
 
1 
RESOLUTION NO. _________ 
 
ENTITLED: “A 
RESOLUTION 
AMENDING 
RESOLUTION 
NO. 
5521 
AND 
AUTHORIZING SUBMISSION OF AN APPLICATION TO THE WYOMING 
BUSINESS COUNCIL, BUSINESS READY COMMUNITY GRANT AND 
LOAN PROGRAM FOR FUNDING THROUGH THE DOWNTOWN 
DEVELOPMENT GRANT AND LOAN PROGRAM FOR AN ELEVATED 
WALKWAY.” 
 
 
WHEREAS, Resolution No. 5521, adopted by the Governing Body at its August 26, 
2013 meeting inadvertently omitted wording with respect to the loan component of the project 
which is the subject of this Resolution; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne desires to participate in the 
Wyoming Business Council Business Ready Community/Downtown Development Program to 
assist Spirit Hospitality, LLC, in constructing an elevated pedestrian access walkway from the 
Jack R. Spiker Parking Facility across Pioneer Avenue to a location within Block 392, Original 
City, City of Cheyenne, 1620 Pioneer Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming (“Block 392”); and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the purposes of the elevated pedestrian access walkway includes providing 
pedestrian access for the general public from the Spiker Parking Facility to the streets, sidewalks, 
and businesses located in and around the 1600 block of Pioneer Avenue and to provide access to 
and from the Spiker Parking Facility for guests of a Hilton Gardens Hotel which Spirit 
Hospitality, LLC, proposes to construct; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the eligibility requirements of the Business Ready Community/Downtown 
Development Program include the provision that a portion of eligible program funds may be 
distributed in the form of a loan to the City of Cheyenne as the grant applicant; and  
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne will request Business Ready Community/Downtown 
Development Program grant funds in the amount of Five Hundred Sixty-Two Thousand Two 
Hundred Forty-Eight Dollars ($562,248.00) and loan funds in the amount of One Hundred 
Eighty-Seven Thousand Five Hundred Dollars ($187,500.00), said loan funds to be re-paid by 
the City of Cheyenne over a twenty (20) year period with simple interest in the amount of one 
half of one percent (.5%) compounded annually; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to said terms and conditions, the annual debt service owed by the 
City of Cheyenne to the Wyoming Business Council will be in the approximate amount of Nine 
Thousand Eight Hundred Seventy-Five Dollars ($9,875.00) annually; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne will secure the loan through a non-exclusive lease of 
the elevated pedestrian access walkway to Spirit Hospitality, LLC, for use by its guests with 
annual lease payments for Years 1 through 4 in the amount of Ten Thousand Dollars 
($10,000.00) and annual lease payments for Years 5 through 20 in the amount of  Fourteen 
Thousand Seven Hundred and Fifty Dollars ($14,750.00); and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the term of said lease must also extend beyond the twenty (20) year loan re-
payment period, described in the preceding paragraph, to ensure that Spirit Hospitality will have 
the non-exclusive right to use the elevated pedestrian access walkway for the entire period of 
time covered by the “Hilton Hotels” franchise agreement for this project, which franchise 
agreement will expire at 11:59 p.m., July 31, 2035; and  
 
 
WHEREAS, in the absence of breach, amendment, or revocation of said non-exclusive 
lease, the annual lease payments for Years 21 through the termination of said lease shall be Four 
Thousand Dollars ($4,000.00) per year; and   
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne will use revenue recaptured through this project, 
estimated at Seventy-Eight Thousand Five Hundred Dollars ($78,500.00) over the twenty (20) 
year loan re-payment period, to support the City of Cheyenne’s West Edge Project; and 

 
 
2 
 
WHEREAS, said grant and loan funds will be used by the City of Cheyenne to construct 
the elevated walkway, which will be retained in the ownership of the City as public pedestrian 
access infrastructure; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the use of grant and loan funds for the construction of an elevated pedestrian 
access walkway satisfies the eligibility criteria which have been established by the Wyoming 
Business Council for the Business Ready Community/Downtown Development Program; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the eligibility requirements of the Business Ready Community/Downtown 
Development Program also include the requirement that the grant applicant provide a grant 
match and the City’s match will be provided through the loan component of the grant application 
as described above and through capital investments of Spirit Hospitality, LLC; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne will follow state procurement standards, inclusive of 
W.S. § 15-1-113 and W.S. § 16-6-101, et seq., in the construction of the elevated pedestrian 
access walkway; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne will comply with the Wyoming Preference Act (W.S. 
§§ 16-6-201 through 16-6-206) and all related state statutes in the construction of the elevated 
pedestrian access walkway; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne recognizes the construction of 
an elevated walkway will provide public benefit; and 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, that a loan application in the amount of One Hundred Eighty-Seven 
Thousand Five Hundred Dollars ($187,500.00), subject to and including the terms and conditions 
set forth above, be submitted to the Wyoming Business Council for the purpose of assisting in 
the funding of the construction of the elevated pedestrian access walkway as described above. 
 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Mayor of the City of Cheyenne, or such other 
City official as may be designated by the Mayor, is authorized to sign an application for this 
grant and loan request and that the Mayor, or his designated representative, is authorized to 
execute a grant agreement and loan documents as required by the Wyoming Business Council in 
the event that grant and loan funds, as described herein, are awarded to the City of Cheyenne for 
this project. 
 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that Resolution No. 5521 is amended to the extent that 
Resolution No. 5521 is inconsistent with this Resolution. 
 
 
ADOPTED THIS __________day of October, 2013. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
____________________________________
______ 
 
 
Richard L. Kaysen, Mayor 
 
(SEAL) 
Attest 
 
____________________________________ 
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2013-10-04_TotalExtraction_24887541.pdf
 
RESOLUTION NO. ________________ 
 
 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR AND THE CITY 
CLERK TO SIGN A FINAL PLAT AND DEVELOPMENT 
AGREEMENT FOR TERRE PONT SUBDIVISION, A PORTION 
OF THE S1/2 OF SECTION 20, T.14N., R.66W., 6TH P.M., 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING (LOCATED AT THE NORTHEAST 
CORNER OF THE INTERSECTION OF PRAIRIE AVENUE AND 
FRONTIER MALL DRIVE).” 
 
 
 
WHEREAS, the owners of the property described herein have subdivided said 
land in accordance with the statutes in such cases made and provided; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the owners of the property described herein have caused a 
subdivision plat of said land to be made, acknowledged, and certified, particularly 
describing the lots, blocks, easements and right-of-way; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the above described subdivision plat has been presented to the City 
of Cheyenne Planning Commission for consideration and the Planning Commission has 
recommended that the Governing Body approve the subdivision plat; and  
 
 
WHEREAS, the above-described plat has been duly executed by the 
Development Office; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the owners of the property described herein and the City wish to 
enter into the attached development agreement addressing the connection of Rue Terre to 
the southeast. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, THAT the subdivision described as 
Terre Pont Subdivision, a portion of the S1/2 of Section 20, T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, be and the same hereby is approved and confirmed as presented, 
and that the Mayor and the City Clerk be and are hereby authorized, empowered, and 
directed to execute said plat when Community Facility Fees have been paid in full, and 
after the attached development agreement has been executed and recorded with the 
Laramie County Clerk. 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS _____________ DAY OF  
 
__________________________, 2013. 
 
 
 
_____________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
RICHARD L. KAYSEN, MAYOR 
 
(SEAL)

UDC-13-00357 – Terre Pont Final Plat – Page 3 
PLAT ANALYSIS SUMMARY: 
In order to address the need for “regional transportation systems with local networks that support 
multiple and alternative routes for daily trips, do not overly burden any single roadway, and include 
logical connections to existing, planned, or potential future streets” (UDC Sec. 4.3.1.d), connection of 
Rue Terre should occur with development of the subject property. The attached development agreement 
requires the connection to be made prior to issuance of a certificate of occupancy for Lot 2, Block 1, of 
Terre Pont Subdivision. Please see below for more information.  
DESCRIPTION OF SITE AND SURROUNDINGS: 
The subject property is approximately 8.45 acres of vacant land northeast of and adjacent to Frontier 
Mall Drive and Prairie Avenue. On the east side of the property is the platted but unbuilt Cutoff Road. 
The land raises several feet from Prairie Avenue, then slopes back down to where Rue Terre aligns. 
Frontier Mall Drive is fully constructed including the sidewalk. Prairie Avenue is built, but the sidewalk 
will need to be constructed as the property develops. Rue Terre and Cutoff Road are not constructed.  
 
Looking northwest on Prairie Avenue. Subject property on right. 
PROJECT DATA: 
This plat proposes two lots; Lot 1 on the west being 3.1 acres and Lot 2 being 3.16 acres. One hundred forty 
feet of right-of-way, or 2.19 acres, will be dedicated to extend Rue Terre. Right-of-way for Cutoff Road is in 
place. Lot 1 is proposed for development of an extended-stay hotel. Rue Terre adjacent to the subdivision will 
be constructed with development of the hotel. The attached development agreement would require the 
remaining unconstructed section between where Cutoff Road is platted and the existing Rue Terre to the 
southeast, north of K-Mart, to be constructed with development of Lot 2.  
At first, both lanes on Rue Terre will use the south half of the right-of-way, the same as it is north of Frontier 

UDC-13-00357 – Terre Pont Final Plat – Page 4 
Mall Drive behind the shopping center there. The other half of the road will be constructed with future 
development to the north. Please the aerial map below for clarification. 
 
PLAT ANALYSIS:  
Review of a Final Plat is organized by analysis of the review criteria described in Final Plat Review 
Criteria outlined in Article 2, Section 2.1.3.d. The purpose of these review criteria is to provide a 
consistent review and discussion for Final Plat applications.  
1 || Preliminary Plat – The layout and design of the Final Plat is in substantial compliance with the 
approved preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and 
access, and any deviations in the Final Plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. 
The Final Plat matches the approved Preliminary Plat.

UDC-13-00357 – Terre Pont Final Plat – Page 5 
2 || Preliminary Plat Conditions – The application meets any conditions or modifications of the 
approved preliminary plat. 
No conditions were placed on the Preliminary Plat. Based on the Transportation Worksheet submitted 
with the Preliminary Plat, a Transportation Impact Study (TIS) was required with the Final Plat 
submittal. Revisions to the submitted TIS were required and submitted on October 4, 2013. Based on 
review of the TIS and discussions at Planning Commission and City Council during the preliminary plat 
process, a condition of connection of Rue Terre is recommended. 
3 || Subdivision Regulations – The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of 
Article 4. 
Article 4 is divided into five sections: 
4.1 General Provisions 
The first specific purpose of Article 4 listed in the UDC is to “Ensure that all development blocks and 
lots are served by necessary infrastructure services, including utilities, transportation, storm drainage, 
public safety, and community facilities, but recognize that necessary service levels may differ based on 
the context, character, and intensity of development.” Utilities and roads are being installed by the 
developer. Development of the property will increase pressure to construct Rue Terre from the subject 
property to where Rue Terre ends between Kmart and Sam’s Club.  
4.2 Community Facilities and Fees 
The Final Plat will require payment of park fees as outlined below (subject to change).

4.3 Transportation Networks and Street Designs 
The proposed development complies with the long-range transportation plan, maximum block perimeter, and 
maximum and minimum block face lengths. The location of the streets surrounding the subject property is 
generally established already. Sidewalks will need to be installed where they do not currently exist. In order to 
address transportation connectivity, Rue Terre needs to be constructed to the south to where it currently ends 
behind K-Mart.  
 

UDC-13-00357 – Terre Pont Final Plat – Page 6 
 
Looking southeast at where Rue Terre ends north of K-Mart. 
 
Looking south on Cutoff Road, east of subject property. 

UDC-13-00357 – Terre Pont Final Plat – Page 7 
 
Looking north at Lot 2, Block 1, Terre Fini north of K-Mart. 
 
Looking southeast from where Rue Terre ends northwest of subject property at Frontier Mall Drive.  
Subject property on right. Back of K-Mart in background. 

UDC-13-00357 – Terre Pont Final Plat – Page 8 
4.4 Open Space Systems 
Open space equivalent to eight percent of the area of the building footprint will be required on-site, which will 
be private open space. This requirement will be evaluated during the Site Plan review. 
4 || Phasing Amenities, Facilities - Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and 
demonstrates a logical and well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of 
amenities and public facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing 
or the likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. 
An extended stay hotel is proposed for Lot 1. Street improvements will be made to Rue Terre the length 
of the entire subdivision (e.g., adjacent to both Lots 1 and 2). No development is proposed for Lot 2 at 
this time. It is recommended that construction of this portion of Rue Terre be required prior to issuance 
of a Certificate of Occupancy (CO) for Lot 2 of the subject property. This can best be accomplished 
through a development agreement between the City and the developer. 
For background information, in order to develop commercial property Site Plan approval is required. 
Once the Site Plan is approved a Building Permit can be issued. In order to occupy the building a 
Certificate of Occupancy (CO) is required, which determines that all Building Code and Site Plan 
requirements have been met. Placing the requirement for construction of the road on the issuance of the 
CO allows the road to be constructed simultaneously with the development of the lot. 
5 || Studies and Reports - The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were 
prepared or requested to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the application, 
can mitigate these impacts. 
The Drainage Study was reviewed and corrections made based on those reviews. A revised Transportation 
Impact Study (TIS) and addendum was submitted October 4, 2013. 
6 || Zoning - All proposed lots shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed zoning 
district standards, and result in site design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district. All parcels not proposed as 
buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the City shall have a clearly defined 
entity responsible for ongoing maintenance. 
Both lots appear to be developable with the proposed CB zoning. 
7 || Adjacent Development - The application will not create any undue influence on existing or 
potential development for adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan 
policies for that property. 
The properties to the south and west are already similarly developed. The proposed development should 
have a positive effect on adjacent vacant land by extending water and sewer lines and street 
improvements. 
8 || Health Safety and Welfare - The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and 
welfare. 
Development will be required to meet all applicable requirements regarding engineering, building 
construction, fire access and hydrants, and food safety, among others. A revised TIS is required in order 
to properly assess impacts of the proposed development on the transportation system and to determine 
improvements necessary to maintain adequate levels of service. While it is not anticipated that the 
revised TIS would cause a major impact on the proposed development, it may affect the discussion 
regarding the extension of Rue Terre to where is currently exists to the east of the subject property. The 

UDC-13-00357 – Terre Pont Final Plat – Page 9 
City Attorney’s office is preparing a draft development agreement regarding Rue Terre’s construction 
timing as well, which will be sent to the applicant when it is available. If the applicant is agreement to 
the terms of the development agreement it will be submitted to the governing body for approval and 
recorded with the Final Plat, if approved. 
REVIEWING AGENCIES AND REVIEWER’S COMMENTS: 
Traffic Engineering: (Note these comments were addressed with a revised TIS and addendum and 
the proposed development agreement.) 
1. A Transportation Impact Study (TIS) is required as the proposed development daily vehicle trip-end 
generation is 1,113 and the peak-hour trip generation in the P.M. Peak is 113, which exceed the 
threshold volumes of 1,000 trip-end and 100 peak-hour trips respectively. We have received and 
reviewed a TIS dated August 5, 2013, by Inberg-Miller Engineers. Our comments are as follows: 
a. Evaluate the sight distance triangles at the intersections of Frontier Mall Drive with Prairie 
Avenue and Rue Terre Drive. 
b. There is a discussion in the TIS under the recommendations that consideration should be given to 
restricting Kohl’s driveway to right-in, right-out. Access management necessary as a result of 
development should be incorporated into the offsite construction. The location of access to 
properties on opposite sides of roadways shall be coordinated so they do not interfere with one 
another. Driveway approaches directly opposite each other are desirable. Every effort should be 
made to coordinate the location of driveways and roadways directly opposite of each other. If 
this is not possible, the resulting “T” configurations shall be spaced a minimum of 100 feet apart 
on collectors and 200 feet on arterials. 
c. The Cheyenne MPO Dell Range Corridor Study does not have any bearing on this project. Any 
infrastructure necessary to make the hotel and restaurant project viable needs to be incorporated 
into this project design and construction. 
d. Crash data and analysis is required as part of a TIS in accordance with Article 3 of the Unified 
Development Code. 
e. It appears that the assumptions contain in the Transportation Worksheet were not followed. The 
Worksheet indicated that trip generation was based off a 5,000 square feet building. The trips 
generated and used in the trip distribution and the analysis appears to have been based off of a 
3,000 square feet building. Please review and revise all analysis accordingly. 
f. An interim condition was analyzed in addition to the traditional analysis horizons. The future 
forecast with development indicates no change in traffic was assumed on Dell Range Boulevard. 
Is the assumption that no traffic will enter the site from Rue Terre via Dell Range? 
g. The analysis at the signalized intersections appears to have been performed with cycle lengths 
that vary from 88 seconds to 220 seconds. This analysis does not reflect the actual cycle lengths 
along this coordinated corridor. I have attached the timing plans for all signalized intersections. 
Please review and revise all analysis accordingly. 
h. All Arterial and Collector streets shall be connected and extended to the boundary of the site. 
i. For adjacent plotted lots or future phase developments within the City along arterials and 
collectors, access points should be combined and shared within blocks. The future phase access 
should be shared with the proposed hotel. 
Engineering: (Note these comments would be addressed with the proposed development 
agreement.) 

UDC-13-00357 – Terre Pont Final Plat – Page 10 
1. When constructed, this project will leave approximately 460 feet of Rue Terre unconstructed behind 
K-Mart. This stretch will probably be difficult to keep from becoming an unofficial road between the 
two constructed sections. This may result in the City having to incur some expense to construct the 
missing piece. 
Board of Public Utilities: 
1. The exiting 8" water main on the west end of Rue Terre will need to be extended from its current 
location to tie into the exiting 12" water on the east end of Rue Terre to complete the water main 
loop. All BOPU's utilities shall be located in the City of Cheyenne Street Right-of-Ways or have the 
appropriate easements record at the Clerk of Laramie County Real Estate Office or easements shown 
on the recorded plat. 
Charter (formerly Optimum): 
1. Charter communications has fiber optics in the road ROW at the northwest corner of the intersection 
of Prairie Ave and Cut Off Road. This fiber line crosses Prairie from the south to this corner then 
crosses Cut Off Road from this corner east to the 10' utility easement along the north property line of 
Lot 1 Block 1 of Terre Fini 2nd Filing. 
City Surveyor: (Note: These comments were addressed with a revised Final Plat map.) 
1. Using the dimension of 700.76' for the northwesterly boundary line, and the overall dimension of 
187.15' on the southerly (tangent) lines of Lots 1 and 2, as provided in the legal description; the 
boundary description closes mathematically and is properly described in the Dedication. 
2. The line segments for the northwesterly boundary line do not properly add up to the total dimension 
of 700.76'. The two dimensions provided add up to 700.76', but the right of way width for Rue Terre 
is 140', not only the 60.35' dimension provided on the map. And I believe the northwesterly 
dimension for Lot 1 should only be 560'+/-, instead of the 640.41' dimension provided. This is 
required to be double checked and the individual line segment dimensions properly corrected and 
added to the map. 
3. On the southerly boundary, the Lot 2 segment of the tangent, the dimension of 179.05' needs to be 
added. This is required. 
Drainage Engineering: (Note: These comments were addressed with a revised Drainage Study.) 
1. The Time of Concentration, Tc, calculations submitted are based on an overland flow equation. This 
equation is not valid once the runoff becomes concentrated, such as in channels, gutters, and/or 
pipes. This equation is also only valid up to lengths of approximately 500 feet and 300 feet, pre- and 
post-project respectively. Please revise Tc calculations to reflect the above criteria, or submit 
justification for alternate methodology.  
2. Rational Method calculations for the City of Cheyenne must include the C-Value Correction 
Coefficient, Cf. This coefficient is 1.0 for storms up to the 25-year, and 1.1, 1.2, and 1.25 for the 25-, 
50-, and 100-year storms. Please revise the Rational Method calculations to include this coefficient, 
or submit justification for alternate methodology.  
3. Please submit complete detention volume calculations. 
4. The City of Cheyenne criteria for detention pond sizing in the Dry Creek drainage basin is based on 
the post-project 100-year peak discharge not exceeding the pre-project 20-year peak discharge. 
Please submit calculations that meet this criterion, or submit justification for alternate methodology. 

UDC-13-00357 – Terre Pont Final Plat – Page 11 
Note: The City Engineer's Office has available a FAA Method spreadsheet that includes the 
information to assist in addressing the above comments. 
GIS: (Note: This comment was addressed with a revised Final Plat map.) 
1. Need block number. 
Fire & Rescue: (Note: This comment shall be addressed with the Site Plan.) 
1. Fire hydrant placement and fire access shall comply with the 2006 edition of the International Fire 
Code Appendix C & D. 
MPO: (Note these comments were addressed with a revised TIS and addendum and the proposed 
development agreement.) 
1. A revised traffic study will be required as the current one submitted is incomplete. i.e., crash data is 
available from WYDOT or the MPO to analyze the current traffic safety of this site and also with 
this development. All necessary access management and traffic control as a result of development 
shall be incorporated into the project. The location of accesses shall follow all development 
regulations as per all UDC subdivision regulations including section 4.3.6. The current Dell Range 
and Prairie Avenue Corridor Study has no bearing on this project. All necessary mitigation of 
impacts that this project will require should be determined and indicated in the design and 
construction documents. 
Urban Planning: (Note: These comments were addressed with a revised Final Plat map.) 
1. Add “Block 1” to map. 
2. In the Dedication delete “are the following owners”.  
3. Replace “TERRE PONT ADDITION” with “TERRE PONT SUBDIVISION” in Dedication and 
Surveyor’s Certificate to match general description in the lower right-hand corner. 
4. ”Casey” is misspelled under the Dedication as “Casy”. 
5. Add “Cheyenne” before “Laramie County” in the lower right-hand corner. 
6. Delete “and Laramie County Regional” in the Approval block. 
7. Replace “Lot 10” with “Lot 7” for “FRONTIER MALL, 3rd FILING”. 
Reviewing Agencies with No Comment:  None 
Reviewing Agencies Not Responding: CenturyLink; Cheyenne Light, Fuel & Power 
Other Comments Received: None

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2013-10-04_TotalExtraction_21759018.pdf
Page 1 of 7 
 
DEVELOPMENT AGREEMENT 
 
 
THIS DEVELOPMENT AGREEMENT (the "Agreement") is made this _____ day of 
October, 2013, by and between the City of Cheyenne, a political subdivision of the State of 
Wyoming, whose address is 2101 O'Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, 82001 (the "City"), and 
Edward George Cole, Casey Lee Cole, Christy Kay Cole, now known as Christy Marziano, 
Kevin Louis Cole, Lester Lee Cole, Ann M. Cole, and their spouses, guardians, conservators, 
trustees, and other representatives and assigns, (hereinafter collectively referred to as “the 
Coles”), 15469 Harvest Mile Road, Brighton, Colorado 80603, and is intended to facilitate the 
development of 8.45 acres of land (containing approximately 368,082 square feet), which land is 
legally described in Exhibit A attached hereto.  
 
WITNESSETH 
 
WHEREAS, the Coles are the owners of  certain real property located within the municipal 
boundaries of the City of Cheyenne City, more particularly described in Exhibit A, and 
hereinafter referred to as the “Development Area;” and  
 
WHEREAS, the Coles have proposed a final plat to divide the Development Area into certain 
lots and blocks to be known as the “Terre Pont Subdivision” which are described and depicted in 
the proposed final plat map, attached hereto as Exhibit B, and fully incorporated herein by 
reference; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City has determined that it is in the best interests of the residents of the City that 
certain roadway improvements adjacent to and near the Development Area be constructed by and 
at the expense of the Coles in connection with the platting and future development of the 
Development Area and that the terms and conditions of this Development Agreement are entered 
into for the purpose of establishing those development requirements as a condition of the 
approval of the final plat for the Terre Pont Subdivision; and  
 
WHEREAS, it is to the mutual benefit of the parties to enter into this Agreement.  
 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual promises, covenants and agreements 
hereinafter contained and other good and valuable consideration, the sufficiency and adequacy of 
which are hereby acknowledged by the parties, the parties hereto agree as follows. 
 
1. 
Development Area. This Agreement covers all of the development area (the 
"Development Area") set out in Exhibit A, attached hereto and incorporated by this reference, all 
of which is owned as of the date of this Agreement by the Coles. 
2. 
Intent of the Parties. It is the intent of the parties to this Agreement to impose certain 
duties and responsibilities on the Coles to construct certain street and related infrastructure 
improvements in connection with the present and future development of the Terre Pont 
Subdivision. The duties and obligations of the Coles as specified herein are intended to constitute 
a covenant which shall run with the land to all successors and assigns of the Coles and for that 
purpose this Development Agreement shall be recorded, at the expense of the Coles, in the books 

Page 2 of 7 
 
and records of the Laramie County Clerk and ex officio register of deeds to provide public notice 
of said covenant. 
3. 
Duration of Agreement. This Agreement shall remain in full force and effect until such 
time as the Coles, and their successors in interest, have complied with all of the duties and 
obligations specified in this Development Agreement with regard to the construction of street 
and related infrastructure improvements as provided for herein, or until such time as the parties 
hereto, or their successors and assigns,  revoke, cancel or amend this Agreement through  a  
written  instrument  executed  with  the same  formalities  as  this Agreement,  whichever occurs 
first.  The parties hereto shall cause any amendment to this Agreement to be recorded in the 
books and records of the Laramie County Clerk. 
4. 
Street Improvements. 
 
(a) The Coles shall, at their expense, and in accordance with City and BOPU specifications, 
construct the south half of the standard street section for Rue Terre, within its platted right-of-
way, from the west edge of Lot 1, Block 1, to the east edge of Lot 2, Block 1 of Terre Pont 
Subdivision. This construction shall be completed prior to the issuance of any Certificate of 
Occupancy for any improvement to be constructed on Lot 1, Block 1 of Terre Pont Subdivision. 
The construction of the street section governed by this paragraph is graphically illustrated in 
Exhibit C attached hereto. 
 
(b) The Coles shall, at their expense and in accordance with City and BOPU specifications, 
construct the south half of the standard street section for Rue Terre, within its platted right-of-
way, from the east edge of the Terre Pont Subdivision to reach and be connected to the existing 
section of Rue Terre which has already been constructed. This construction shall be completed 
prior to the issuance of any Certificate of Occupancy for any improvement to be constructed on 
Lot 2, Block 1 of Terre Pont Subdivision. In the event the City pays for the construction of this 
section of Rue Terre before development of Lot 2, Block 1 of Terre Pont Subdivision, the entire 
costs of said construction shall be allocated, as a development cost, to Lot 2, Block 1 of Terre 
Pont Subdivision and said costs shall be paid by the Coles, or their successors in interest to said 
Lot and Block, prior to the issuance of any building permit for any improvement on said Lot and 
Block. In the event a developer other than the City pays to construct this section of Rue Terre, 
the provisions of Chapter 1.16 of the Cheyenne City Code shall apply. The construction of the 
street section governed by this paragraph is graphically illustrated in Exhibit C attached hereto.  
5. 
Other Improvements. The terms and conditions of this Development Agreement are not 
intended and shall not be construed to constitute a full and complete itemization of 
improvements which may be required by the City and/or the City of Cheyenne Board of Public 
Utilities ("BOPU") in connection with the development of Lot 1, Block 1, Lot 2, Block 1, Terre 
Pont Subdivision, or any adjoining property owned or controlled by the Coles or their successors 
and assigns. Extensions of water, sanitary sewer, storm sewer, road improvements, and other 
items identified by the City and/or BOPU may be required in addition to the terms and 
conditions of this Development Agreement. The Coles, and their successors and assigns, shall 
comply with all standard policies, procedures, and requirements of the City and BOPU regarding 
on-and off-site improvements.  

Page 3 of 7 
 
6. 
Cooperation. The parties agree that they will cooperate with one another in 
accomplishing the terms, conditions and provisions of this Agreement and will execute such 
additional documents as are reasonably necessary to effectuate the same. 
7. 
Amendments. Any changes, modifications, revisions or amendments to this Agreement 
that are mutually agreed upon in writing by the parties hereto shall be incorporated by written 
instrument, signed by all parties to this Agreement. 
8. 
Notices. All notices arising out of, or from, the provisions of this Agreement shall be in 
writing and given to the parties at the addresses set out above by certified mail, return receipt 
requested, with copy to: Edward George Cole, et al., 15469 Harvest Mile Road, Brighton, 
Colorado 80603. 
9. 
Remedies and Enforcement. In the event the Coles or the City defaults or is deficient in 
the performance of any term of this Agreement, the Coles or the City shall have the right to 
exercise all remedies provided by law or in equity. 
10. 
Third Party Beneficiaries. It is expressly understood and agreed that enforcement of the 
terms and conditions of this Agreement, and all rights of action relating to such enforcement, 
shall be strictly reserved to the City and the Coles, and nothing contained in this Agreement shall 
give or allow any such claim or right of action by any other third party on this Agreement. It is 
the expressed intention of the City and the Coles that any person other than the City and the 
Coles receiving services or benefits under this Agreement shall be deemed to be an incidental 
beneficiary only. 
11. 
Waiver. A waiver by any party to this Agreement or the breach of any term or provision 
of this Agreement shall not operate or be construed as a waiver of any subsequent breach by 
either party. 
12. 
Successors and Assigns. As used in this Agreement, the term "the Coles” and "City" shall 
include all successors, transferees, and assigns of such parties, and all such parties  shall  have  
the  right to enforce this Agreement and shall be subject to the terms of this Agreement as if they 
were the original parties hereto. 
13. 
Severability. Should any portion of this Agreement be judicially determined to be illegal 
or unenforceable, the remainder of the Agreement shall continue in full force and effect as if the 
illegal or unenforceable term was omitted, except that the breach of a condition precedent shall 
void this Agreement through lack of formation. 
14. 
Governmental Immunity. The City does not waive governmental immunity or any other 
defenses provided by law and specifically retains all immunities and defenses available pursuant 
to W.S. § l- 39-101 et seq. and all other State laws. 
15. 
Kickbacks. The Coles warrant that no gratuities, kickbacks, or contingency fees were 
paid in connection with this Agreement, nor were any fees, commissions, gifts, or other 
considerations made contingent upon the award of this Agreement.  

Page 4 of 7 
 
16. 
Entirety of the Agreement. This Agreement represents the entire and integrated 
agreement between the parties and supersedes all prior negotiations and agreements, whether 
written or oral. 
17. 
Effective Date. This Agreement shall be effective upon filing with the Laramie County 
Clerk and ex officio register of deeds. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties hereto have executed this Development Agreement on the 
date first above written. 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING (SEAL) 
 
 
 
 
 
By: 
 
 Richard L. Kaysen, Mayor 
 
ATTEST:

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2013-10-04_TotalExtraction_75989132.pdf

RESOLUTION NO. __________
ENTITLED:
“A RESOLUTION APPROVING ‘THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING,
COMPENSATION AND CLASSIFICATION STUDY, FINAL REPORT,’ DATED
OCTOBER 1, 2013, PREPARED BY THE MERCER GROUP, INC., AND
REFERRING SAID STUDY TO THE ‘EMPLOYEE INVESTMENT STUDY
IMPLEMENTATION TEAM’ CREATED BY RESOLUTION NO. 5508.” 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne engaged the Mercer Group, Inc., to conduct an employee
investment study to determine appropriate staffing levels and pay ranges for the various departments
and classifications of City employees; and
WHEREAS, the employee investment study consists of several components including the
formulation of a Recommended General Pay Plan, a Recommended Police Pay Plan, and a statement
of Recommended Position Titles by Pay Grade; and
WHEREAS, the Mercer Group, Inc., has presented “The City of Cheyenne, Wyoming,
Compensation and Classification Study, Final Report,” dated October 1, 2013, which addresses
those components of the employee investment study described in the preceding paragraph and which
is attached to this Resolution as Exhibit A; and
WHEREAS, pursuant to Resolution No. 5508, adopted July 22, 2013, the Governing Body
has created the “Employee Investment Study Implementation Team” to consider alternative means
of implementing the recommendations of the Compensation and Classification Study which is the
subject of this Resolution and the staffing study which is anticipated to be received on or before
October 23, 2013; and
WHEREAS, pursuant to Resolution No. 5508, a final plan containing alternative means of
implementing the recommendations of the Compensation and Classification Study, together with
other recommendations to be received by October 23, 2013, must be submitted by the “Employee
Investment Study Implementation Team” to the Governing Body no later than January 1, 2014, or
such other date as may be determined by the Governing Body, for possible implementation during
the Fiscal Year 2015 Budget; and
WHEREAS, this Resolution shall not affect and shall not be construed as affecting: (1)  the
City’s FY 2014 budget as approved and amended by the Governing Body; (2) the employment terms
and conditions of any employee currently employed by the City of Cheyenne or the Cheyenne Police
Department; (3) the employment terms and conditions of any member of the International
Association of Firefighters, Local No. 279; (4) any provision, term or condition of City Ordinance
No. 3980, approved June 24, 2013, pertaining to non-uniformed employees of the City of Cheyenne;
or (5) to any provision, term or condition of City Ordinance No. 3981, approved June 24, 2013,
pertaining to sworn officers of the Cheyenne Police Department.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Governing Body approves “The City of Cheyenne,
Wyoming, Compensation and Classification Study, Final Report,” dated October 1, 2013, attached
hereto as Exhibit A.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that “The City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, Compensation and
Classification Study, Final Report,” dated October 1, 2013, is hereby referred to the “Employee
Investment Study Implementation Team” to consider the recommendations of said Compensation
and Classification Study and to prepare a plan containing alternative means of implementing the
recommendations of said study, together with such other recommendations as shall be forthcoming
upon final completion of the employee investment study, said implementation plan to be presented
to the Governing Body no later than January 1, 2014, or such other date as may be determined by
the Governing Body.

2
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS ______ DAY OF _______________, 2013.
__________________________________________
Richard L. Kaysen, Mayor
(Seal)

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2013-10-04_TotalExtraction_54793542.pdf
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
 
COMPENSATION AND 
CLASSIFICATION STUDY 
 
FINAL REPORT  
 
 
 
October 1, 2013 
 
 
 
 
 
THE MERCER GROUP, INC. 

____________________________________________________________________________________________ 
Cheyenne, Wyoming Final Report 
 
Classification and Compensation Study 
 
The Mercer Group, Inc. 
October 1, 2013 
 
Page 2 
 
 
 
COMPENSATION AND CLASSIFICATION STUDY  
DRAFT FINAL REPORT  
 
I.  BACKGROUND 
 
The Mercer Group, Inc. was retained by the City to conduct a Classification and Compensation 
Study. 
The City was seeking the services of a human resources consultant to conduct a classification and 
compensation study to update the City’s current pay structure to ensure that the City is 
competitive with the market. 
II.  METHODOLOGY 
The Mercer Group, Inc., Inc. methodology for classification and compensation studies is a 
combination of specific internal and external data which places the salary schedule into parity 
with the market and each position into parity with all other positions.  From this internal and 
external data pay levels are determined, positions are classified and placed into the salary 
schedule, and ongoing methods of maintaining the system are developed. 
Detailed descriptions of the methodologies are described below. 
 
A.  Compensation/Market Survey Methodology 
 
 
The Compensation/Market Survey is a data-collection process which ensures a fair and 
equitable comparison of the pay structure.   
 
Usually, the core of this process is the comparison of the City‘s pay plan with the pay 
plans of comparable organizations.  The City of Cheyenne’s pay plan, being over 17 years 
old, was of no use as a comparison model.  As such, a new pay plan for non-public safety 
employees was developed using predominately market data. 
The primary objective of the market survey and analysis is to determine how the 
compensation levels for the City‘s job classes compare to that of similar public employers 
with whom the City must compete for employees. 
The results of the market survey were used to construct a pay plan that will ensure the 
ability of the City to recruit and retain qualified employees and to provide an effective 
basis for managing salary costs. 
 

____________________________________________________________________________________________ 
Cheyenne, Wyoming Final Report 
 
Classification and Compensation Study 
 
The Mercer Group, Inc. 
October 1, 2013 
 
Page 3 
 
 
 
Market Survey Employer Selection 
The employers selected for the market survey should define as accurately as possible the 
“Labor Market” within which the City competes.  Organizations were selected using the 
following criteria: 
Geographic Proximity - Comparison organizations located within a reasonable 
commuting distance were selected.  Selecting employers in relatively close geographic 
proximity ensures that the market will reflect the City’s cost of living, growth rate and 
other demographic characteristics. 
Nature of Services Provided – Organizations were selected which provide a similar range 
of services, these organizations are most likely to compete with each other for employees, 
likely to have comparable jobs, and are likely to have similar organizational structures.   
Each of these factors was taken into consideration in the identification of an appropriate 
labor market for the City.  City management assisted in the identification of market 
organizations. 
The organizations from which survey data was obtained are: 
• LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING 
• LARIMER COUNTY, COLORADO 
• WELD COUNTY, COLORADO 
• LOVELAND, COLORADO 
• GREELEY, COLORADO 
• CASPER, WYOMING 
• LARAMIE, WYOMING 
• SHERIDAN, WYOMING 
• WYOMING STATE GOVERNMENT 
• SCOTTS BLUFF, NEBRASKA 
• LONGMONT, COLORADO 
Of the thirteen organizations identified to be surveyed, all but two completed surveys. 
The only organizations who declined to complete a Market Survey were Fort Collins, 
Colorado, and the federal government. 
Survey Benchmark Position Selection 
The second step in preparing for the market survey is the identification and selection of 
Benchmark positions.  The Benchmark positions are positions contained in the City’s 
classification system for which adequate market matches can be found.  Benchmark 

____________________________________________________________________________________________ 
Cheyenne, Wyoming Final Report 
 
Classification and Compensation Study 
 
The Mercer Group, Inc. 
October 1, 2013 
 
Page 4 
 
 
 
positions provide a reference point for developing salary recommendations for all non-
Benchmark positions. 
Benchmark positions are those which meet the following criteria: 
o Well-defined positions presumed to exist within the local government and private 
organizations which are to be surveyed; 
o Encompass a variety of skill levels within the organization; 
o Reasonably well known and understood positions which are clearly and concisely 
described; 
o Represent a variety of pay levels within the organization. 
 
 
The Benchmark positions selected for the comparison survey are listed below: 
 
• ACCOUNTANT 
• ACCOUNTING TECHNICIAN 
• ATTORNEY 
• CHIEF BUILDING INSPECTOR 
• CONSTRUCTION INSPECTOR I 
• COURT BAILIFF 
• CUSTODIAN (ENTRY LEVEL) 
• CUSTODIAN, SENIOR 
• DEPUTY COURT CLERK 
• ENGINEER 
• ENGINEERING TECHNICIAN 
• EQUIPMENT OPERATOR (ENTRY LEVEL) 
• EXECUTIVE SECRETARY 
• FIRE CHIEF 
• FIRE SAFETY TECHNICIAN 
• FLEET MAINTENANCE DIRECTOR 
• FOREMAN  
• FOREMAN, SENIOR 

____________________________________________________________________________________________ 
Cheyenne, Wyoming Final Report 
 
Classification and Compensation Study 
 
The Mercer Group, Inc. 
October 1, 2013 
 
Page 5 
 
 
 
• GIS COORDINATOR 
• HUMAN RESOURCES ASST. DIRECTOR 
• HUMAN RESOURCES SPECIALIST 
• INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY DIRECTOR 
• MECHANIC, SENIOR 
• NETWORK ADMINISTRATOR 
• OFFICE MANAGER 
• PLANNER (ENTRY LEVEL) 
• PLANNER, SENIOR 
• POLICE CHIEF 
• POLICE OFFICER 
• PUBLIC WORKS DIRECTOR 
• PURCHASING DIRECTOR 
• RECORDS SUPERVISOR 
• RECREATION PROGRAM COORDINATOR 
• SERGEANT 
• TRAFFIC SIGNAL TECHNICIAN 
• TRANSIT BUS DRIVER 
• TRANSIT DISPATCHER 
 
 
It is not possible to conduct an adequate market survey for all positions within the City.  
No organization is structured the same way the City is structured.  Therefore, the City will 
have unique positions that cannot be found in the market; tying classifications only to 
market does not take into account the priorities of the City.  By combining the market 
survey data with the point factor system described below, both external equity and 
internal salary relationships are reflected in the resulting salary setting process. 
 
B.  Classification Methodology 
The classification process began with orientation.  Each position Department Director had 
an individual orientation session to review their department structure, discuss areas of 
concern, and review the positions in their organization.  

____________________________________________________________________________________________ 
Cheyenne, Wyoming Final Report 
 
Classification and Compensation Study 
 
The Mercer Group, Inc. 
October 1, 2013 
 
Page 6 
 
 
 
The position description questionnaires, discussions with Department Directors, and 
insight from human resources were used to ensure a thorough understanding of each 
position.  That understanding is critical, as it becomes the basis for the rating of each 
position using the Factor Evaluation System. 
The Mercer Group, Inc. Factor Evaluation System (FES) is used to provide a numerical 
rating for all positions within the City that were included in the study. 
Factor evaluation systems provide a method of classifying positions based on the work 
performed.  The systems generally are easy to understand and apply and can be used to 
classify and re-classify both permanent and part-time positions. 
In developing the factor system used by the Mercer Group, Inc., Inc. the following 
principles are applied: 
• Important elements of each job should be measured by consistent factors. 
• The factors must be explainable and clearly defined. 
• Factors should relate to observable and measurable aspects of the job. 
• Factors should relate to the business of the organization, i.e. local government. 
• The number of factors should be limited for administrative ease but should 
reflect all aspects of the positions rated. 
The factors used in the Mercer FES are: 
 
 
Factor 1 - Knowledge Required: 
Assigns points based on skills, knowledge, and educational level required by the 
job. 
 
Factor 2 - Supervisory Controls: 
Points are assigned based on the nature and extent of direct supervision required 
over the position. 
 
Factor 3 - Supervision Exercised: 
Measures the amount of supervision or management exercised by the position. 
 
Factor 4 - Guidelines: 
Measures the nature of existing guidelines and the judgment needed to apply 
them. 
 
 

____________________________________________________________________________________________ 
Cheyenne, Wyoming Final Report 
 
Classification and Compensation Study 
 
The Mercer Group, Inc. 
October 1, 2013 
 
Page 7 
 
 
 
Factor 5 - Complexity: 
Measures the nature, number, variety and intricacy of tasks, steps or processes in 
the work performed by the position. 
 
Factor 6 - Scope and Effect: 
Measures the nature of the work and its effect both inside and outside the 
organization. 
 
Factor 7 - Personal Contacts: 
Measures contacts with co-workers, the public and others required in performing 
the duties of the position. 
 
Factor 8 - Purpose of Contacts: 
Measures the level of informational exchange in the personal contacts identified 
in Factor 7. 
 
Factor 9 - Physical Demands: 
Includes physical characteristics and abilities needed as well as the level of 
exertion required in performing the duties of the position. 
 
Factor 10 - Work Environment: 
Considers the risks and discomforts in the physical surroundings. 
 
 
Every position included in the study for the City was rated using the system.  Those 
ratings were then used to determine where each position fit in the salary schedule.  This 
process provided for internal equity.   
III.  FINDINGS 
 
Through analysis and consultation with management, an extensive list of comparable 
organizations was chosen to be surveyed regarding the minimum, midpoint, and maximum salary 
offered for the Benchmark positions.  The Mercer Group, Inc. was able to obtain information 
from 11 comparable organizations.  Using this survey data, The Mercer Group, Inc. identified an 
overall pay scale against which the City is competing for all employees. (No individual 
employee’s pay is determined by this survey data, nor is it accurate to compare an 
employee’s actual salary with individual or aggregate salary information obtained for a 
particular Benchmark position.) As the market for sworn law enforcement positions was 
markedly different from the market for general employees two pay plans were developed. 

____________________________________________________________________________________________ 
Cheyenne, Wyoming Final Report 
 
Classification and Compensation Study 
 
The Mercer Group, Inc. 
October 1, 2013 
 
Page 8 
 
 
 
The new pay ranges in both plans place the City at the approximate average of the market 
overall; i.e. half of the organizations surveyed pay more on average while the other half of the 
organizations pay less on average.

IV.  IMPLEMENTATION COSTS 
Increasing Employee Salaries to the Minimum of Their New Range 
The priority implementation cost will be incurred in bringing any employee whose current salary 
is below the minimum of their new pay grade up to that minimum.   
The projected cost to bring employees covered by the General Pay Plan up to their new minimum 
is $657,294.61. 
The projected cost to bring employees covered by the Police Pay Plan up to their new minimum 
is $10,771.90. 
 
 
 
 
 
V.  SPECIAL PROVISIONS AND RECOMMENDATIONS 
Following extensive discussions, The Mercer Group, Inc., offers the following provisions and 
recommendations: 
A. Hiring Offer 
We recommend allowing hiring for applicants, with qualifications exceeding the 
stated minimum for the position, up to the Midpoint of the salary grade with a 
recommendation from the affected Department Director that is endorsed by the 
Human Resources Director and approved by the Mayor. 

____________________________________________________________________________________________ 
Cheyenne, Wyoming Final Report 
 
Classification and Compensation Study 
 
The Mercer Group, Inc. 
October 1, 2013 
 
Page 20 
 
 
 
B. Annual Market Adjustment 
We recommend the City make annual adjustments to the Pay Plan based on annual 
analysis of the Consumer Price Index for Southeast Wyoming.  Upon receipt of this 
data, the Governing Body would vote annually on the applicability and the fiscal 
ability of the City to apply a market adjustment to the salary structure. 
C.  Employee Promotions or Transfers 
We recommend that if an employee is promoted to a position in a higher classification 
(grade), the employee will receive the minimum rate of pay for the new pay grade or a 
salary increase of 6%, whichever is greater. 
  
D.  Reclassification 
 
When a new position is created or there is a need for a reclassification of an existing 
position, a Position Questionnaire (as used in the Study) should be completed.  The 
Position questionnaire should then be evaluated using the Factor Evaluation System 
to determine the Pay Grade, thus continuing to maintain the internal equity of 
positions.   
 
E. Maintenance of the Compensation and Classification System 
 
In order to effectively maintain the system as implemented we recommend the 
following time table: 
 
Re-survey the market and adjust the pay plans accordingly every two to three years. 
 
Complete a full compensation and classification study every five to seven years.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2013-10-04_TotalExtraction_71691590.pdf
 
 
1 
RESOLUTION NO. _________ 
 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING SUBMISSION OF AN APPLICATION TO 
THE WYOMING BUSINESS COUNCIL, BUSINESS READY COMMUNITY 
GRANT 
AND 
LOAN 
PROGRAM 
FOR 
FUNDING 
FOR 
THE 
CONSTRUCTION OF A RAIL SPUR WITHIN THE SWAN RANCH 
INDUSTRIAL PARK.” 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne wishes to participate in the 
Wyoming Business Council Business Ready Community/Downtown Development Program to 
assist Granite Peak Development, LP, (“Developer”) in the construction of a rail spur, 
approximately 7.2 miles in length, within the Swan Ranch Industrial Park, Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the purpose of the 7.2 mile rail spur is to connect existing internal rail 
facilities which have been constructed by the Developer adjacent to the Burlington 
Northern/Santa Fe Railroad rail line, which is located generally east and south of the rail park, to 
internal rail facilities which are currently being constructed by the Developer adjacent to the 
Union Pacific Railroad rail line, which is located generally west and north of the rail park; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the eligibility requirements of the Business Ready Community Program  
include the provision that a portion of eligible program funds may be distributed in the form of a 
loan to the City of Cheyenne as the grant applicant; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne may file a Business Committed application for 
grant/loan funds under the Business Ready Community Program of the Wyoming Business 
Council in a total amount not to exceed Six Million Dollars ($6,000,000.00), subject to further 
negotiations between the City and Granite Peak, LP, regarding the drafting and execution of a  
Contingency and Development Agreement setting forth the specific duties and obligations of the 
parties in the event grant and/or loan funds are awarded pursuant to said application; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, in the event loan funds are provided to the City of Cheyenne pursuant to 
said grant application, the City will secure the loan through the ownership of the steel rails and 
ties to be installed during construction of the rail spur until such time as the full amount of the 
loan and accrued interest are paid in full, and through lease revenues to be paid by Granite Peak, 
LP, for use of the rail spur until the loan and accrued interest are paid in full, and through the 
payment of additional interest to the City of Cheyenne to provide consideration for the receipt of 
grant and/or loan funds as required by applicable law; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne will use revenue recaptured through this project for 
economic development purposes in accordance with applicable rules and regulations of the 
Wyoming Business Council; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, it is anticipated, pending the final drafting and execution of a Contingency 
and Development Agreement between the parties, that the use of grant and loan funds for the 
construction of a rail spur within the Swan Ranch Industrial Park will satisfy the eligibility 
criteria which have been established by the Wyoming Business Council for the Business Ready 
Community Program; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne will follow state procurement standards, inclusive of 
W.S. § 15-1-113 and W.S. § 16-6-101, et seq., in the construction of the rail spur; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne will comply with the Wyoming Preference Act (W.S. 
§§ 16-6-201 through 16-6-206) and all related state statutes in the construction of the rail spur; 
and 
 
 
 
 

 
 
2 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne recognizes the construction of 
a rail spur will provide public benefit in that the rail spur will foster the location and 
development of manufacturing and light industrial facilities within the Swan Ranch Industrial 
Park which will provide jobs and diversify the property tax base of the City of Cheyenne and the 
County of Laramie. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, that the Mayor and the City Clerk are hereby authorized to file a 
Business Committed application to the Wyoming Business Council under Business Ready 
Community grant/loan program for the purpose of seeking an award of grant and/or loan funds, 
subject to the drafting and execution of a Contingency and Development Agreement to be 
reviewed and approved by the Governing Body. 
 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Mayor of the City of Cheyenne, or such other 
City official as may be designated by the Mayor, is authorized to sign an application for this 
grant and loan request and that the Mayor, or his designated representative, is authorized to 
execute a grant agreement and loan documents as required by the Wyoming Business Council in 
the event that grant and loan funds, as described herein, are awarded to the City of Cheyenne for 
this project. 
 
 
ADOPTED THIS __________day of __________________, 2013. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
 
Richard L. Kaysen, Mayor 
 
(SEAL) 
Attest 
 
____________________________________ 
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk

WITNESSETH: 
 
WHEREAS, the Department is a law enforcement agency serving the City of Cheyenne, and 
is a department thereof; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Department has received a grant of funds from the Wyoming Division of 
Victim Services for the purpose of providing crime victim assistance services; and 
 
WHEREAS, in order to provide crime victim assistance services, the Department desires to 
retain the services of a “Victim Assistance Advocate;” and 
 
WHEREAS, because funding a Victim Assistance Advocate position is dependent on the 
receipt of a grant, the Department wishes to create a contract employee rather than permanent 
employee position; and 
 
WHEREAS, McInelly has training and experience in the field of assisting crime victims; and 
 
WHEREAS, McInelly desires to provide services to the Department under the terms and 
conditions hereinafter contained. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the promises and mutual covenants herein, the 
parties agree as follows: 
 
1. 
The term of this Agreement shall commence July 1, 2013, and terminate June 30, 
2014, unless sooner terminated pursuant to Section 9 below or if grant funding is no longer available. 
 
2. 
McInelly will be considered a contract employee of the Department.  She will act as a 
Victim Assistance Advocate and assist the Department. 
 
3. 
McInelly shall at all times coordinate her activities hereunder with the Department 
and shall work under the direction of the Department. 
 
4. 
The Department agrees to pay McInelly the sum of Two Thousand Two Hundred 
Fifteen Dollars and Forty-Three Cents ($2,215.43) per month.  The total amount over the term of this 
agreement will not, in any event, exceed the sum of Twenty-Six  Thousand Five Hundred Eighty-
Five Dollars and Sixteen ($26,585.16). 
 
5. 
The Department shall provide McInelly the benefits provided to full-time employees 
of the City of Cheyenne.  The City of Cheyenne will withhold appropriate federal income and social 
security taxes and remit them to the federal government.   
 
6. 
In addition to the compensation set forth above, the Department agrees to pay 
McInelly for hours worked in excess of forty hours in a work week at time and one-half rate. 
 
7. 
In the event that the governing body authorizes a bonus to be paid to city employees, 
McInelly would be eligible to receive a bonus similar to other city employees. Such bonus payment 
shall first be made out of grant funds, if available, then out of Department funds, if grant funds are 
not available. 

 
 
2 
 
8. 
McInelly is subject to the City of Cheyenne Personnel Rules and Regulations, all 
federal and state laws, city ordinances and actions of the city’s governing body pertaining generally 
to city employees, except that McInelly shall not be entitled to the procedural protections set forth in 
the following sections of the City’s personnel rules: Chapter XII, Section 3.B, last sentence; Chapter 
XII, Section 3.C.1, second paragraph; Chapter XII, Section 3.C.2; Chapter XII, Section 3.D; Chapter 
XIII; and Chapter XIV, Section 2.B-D.  Because this contract is for one year and because the funding 
for the position is dependent on grant funding, McInelly is not a permanent employee of the City of 
Cheyenne.  It is mutually understood and agreed that McInelly is employed on an “at will” basis and 
that her employment may be terminated by the Department, with or without cause.   
 
9. 
This Agreement may be terminated by the Department, with or without cause. 
 
10. 
McInelly agrees to comply with all applicable federal, state and local laws, rules and 
regulations in the performance of this Agreement. 
 
11. 
If any provision, section, subsection, sentence, clause or phrase of this Agreement is 
invalidated by any court of competent jurisdiction, the Agreement will automatically terminate. 
 
12. 
All parties acknowledge full opportunity for consultation with legal counsel regarding 
this Agreement, or have consulted with legal counsel. 
 
13. 
The City of Cheyenne and the Department do not waive their sovereign immunity by 
entering into this Agreement and fully retain all immunities and defenses provided by law in respect 
to any action based on or occurring as a result of this Agreement. 
 
14. 
This Agreement contains the entire understanding of the parties.  There are no other 
terms or conditions, written or oral, concerning or controlling this matter.  Time is of the essence 
hereof. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties have hereunder set their hands and seals the day and 
year first above written. 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE 
 
BY: 
__________________________________________ 
Richard L. Kaysen, Mayor 
(SEAL) 
 
ATTEST: 
____________________________________ 
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk 
 
 
CHEYENNE POLICE DEPARTMENT 
 
BY:

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2013-10-04_TotalExtraction_25043987.pdf
MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING
Approved as to
foj
Date
This Memorandum of Understanding ("MOU") is made and entered into this
day
of
, 2013, by the Friends of the Cheyenne Botanic Garden, a non-profit
corporation organized under the laws of the State of Wyoming ("Foundation"), and the City of
Cheyenne, amunicipal corporation organized underthelawsoftheStateof Wyoming, 2101 O'Neil
Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming ("City").
SECTION ONE. INTRODUCTION
The Friends of the Cheyenne Botanic Garden have established a Foundation to provide
financial support for future development, expansion, and enhancement of the Cheyenne Botanic
Gardens. The purposeofthis MOU is to summarizethe mutual understandings of the Foundation
and the City regarding future gifts which may be made by the Foundation to the City for the
purposes stated herein. This MOU shall be made a part ofthe Foundation's records and is intended
as a guide to those who will administer the Foundation in the future.
SECTION TWO. DESCRIPTION OF FOUNDATION PURPOSES
The Foundationmay engagein any fund-raising activitythat is permitted under local, state
and federal law for the purpose of increasing the principal of various endowment accounts which
may be established by the Foundation to support the future development, expansion and
enhancement of the Cheyenne Botanic Gardens. The Foundation represents to the Citythat it is
qualified as a charitable organization and that donors are or will be entitled to charitable contribution
taxdeductions in connectionwith giftstotheFoundationunderapplicableprovisions ofthe Internal
Revenue Code.
SECTION THREE. NAMING RIGHTS
The Foundationmay accept gifts from donors in exchangefor special recognition for such
gifts in the formof "naming"rights to certain facilities, structures, areas, programs, or activities of
the Cheyenne Botanic Gardens. Gifts that are given in exchange for "naming" rights shall be
deposited intoaseparate endowment fundthatshallbemanaged byaseparate Executive Committee
consisting of the President of the Foundation, the Foundation Treasurer, the Director of the
Cheyenne Botanic Gardens, theDirector oftheCityofCheyenne Parks andRecreation Department,
and a member of the Governing Bodyof the City of Cheyenne designated by the President of the
Cheyenne CityCouncil. The Executive Committee shall review all naming rights proposals and
shall present its recommendations regarding any such proposals to the Mayor of the City of
Cheyenne. No naming rights proposal shall be implemented by the City in the absence of the
approval oftheproposal bytheExecutive Committee andtheMayor. Anaming rightsproposal that
has been approved by the Executive Committee shall constitute a "gift instrument" as defined in
W.S. § 17-7-302(a)(iii).

SECTION FOUR. ADMINISTRATION OF "NAMING RIGHTS" GIFTS
A.
All funds derived from the sale of naming rights shall be deposited and invested by
the Foundation in a separate, earmarked, and dedicated "naming rights" endowment fund.
B.
Subject to the terms and conditions of naming rights proposals approved under
Section Three of the MOU, it is mutually understood and agreed that Wyo. Stat. §§ 17-7-301, et
seq., limit the discretion of the Executive Committee with regard to the liquidation or disposition
of principal placed in the endowment fund to be created pursuant to this section of this MOU.
C.
Subject to the terms and conditions ofapproved naming rights proposals and subject
to the discretion ofthe Executive Committee, income generated from this endowment fund shall be
added to the principal of the endowment fund or disbursed to the City to support development,
design, construction or enhancement ofnew structures, facilities, areas, programs or activities ofthe
Cheyenne Botanic Gardens.
D.
Subject to the terms and conditions of approved naming rights proposals, income
derived from this endowment fund shall not be used to defray routine operating expenses of the
Cheyenne Botanic Gardens unless specifically approved by the Executive Committee.
E.
Except as specifically provided by the terms and conditions of approved naming
rights proposals, subject to applicable statutory restrictions on the disposition of principal, and
subject to the discretion ofthe Executive Committee, funds derived from the sale ofnaming rights
may also be used in one or more of the following ways:
1.
To complete construction of the expansion and renovation of the Botanic
Gardens greenhouse and conservatory if City funds are insufficient for this purpose due to
circumstances beyond the control of the City.
2.
After completion of the expansion and renovation of the Botanic Gardens
greenhouse and conservatory, to pay for out-of-pocket expenses ofthe City incidental to the
implementation of a naming rights proposal, such as required signage, forms, and
advertising.
SECTION FIVE. RECOGNITION
In recognition of any gifts made to the Foundation pursuant to an approved naming rights
proposal, the City agrees to attach, affix, or place signs or plaques to recognize the donor's
contributions to facilities, structures, areas, programs, or activities ofthe Cheyenne Botanic Gardens
as specified in the naming rights proposal and to provide donor recognition in print or electronic
media produced by the City, and may include without limitation, the City's website, the City's
"Facebook" and "Twitter" sites, and maps, pamphlets, or guides which publicize the Cheyenne
Botanic Gardens.

SECTION SIX. BINDING OBLIGATIONS
This MOU shall not become binding upon the City of Cheyenne until approved by the
Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne and executed by the Mayor and City Clerk. This MOU
may be terminated by the City, at any time, with or without cause, upon thirty (30) days' written
notice. The City's obligations under any naming rights proposal which has been approved by the
GoverningBody of the City ofCheyenne prior to the effective date ofthe termination ofthis MOU
shall survive the termination of this MOU and shall remain in full force and effect as provided for
in any such approved naming rights proposal.
SECTION SEVEN. GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY
The City of Cheyenne retains all immunities and defenses available under the Wyoming
Governmental Claims Act, W.S. §§ 1-39-101, et seq.
SECTION EIGHT. MISCELLANEOUS
A.
Governing Law. This MOU shall be governed by the laws of State of Wyoming.
B.
Amendment. This MOU may be amended at any time by written agreement signed
by all parties.
C.
Effective Date. The effective date ofthis MOU shall be the date it is fully executed
bythe parties following the approval ofthis MOU by the Governing Body ofthe City ofCheyenne.
FRIENDS OF THE CHEYENNE BOTANIC
GARDEN, INC.
By:
Date
Date
(SEAL)
Attest:
Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk
Signature of Officer
Name of Officer
Title of Officer
CITY OF CHEYENNE
Richard L. Kaysen, Mayor


--- END OF FILE ---
